LATCHFORD.

Shortly before two o'clock on the afternoon of C Tont- tion Day, Wash-Isme presented an animated appearance, when the local celebrations opened with a procers,on. First came the Latchford Brass Band, playing martial and patriotic airs followed by the members of the Coronation Committee, and a strong muster of the Ancient Order of Druids in full regalia. Then came the coaveyances, each containing a crowd of juveniles. Lurries were kindly provided by Messrs Peter Walker and son (2), Messrs Pierpoint and Bryant (2), 'Mr James Ashley (2), and Mr Thomas Ford (1). Following these came Mr Jackson's broug- ham, gaily decorated with Union Jacks, and the scholars and adult inhabitants walking four abreast brought up the rear. The route taken was from Wash-lane to Victoria Park gates. where the pro- cession turned back to Kautsford-road railway arch ; thence to the field kindly lent for the occasion by Mr Thomas Hewitt, via Thelwall-lane and Lower Wash-lane. On the field the following sports were indulged in :- 100 yards race for boys under 10, 50 yards race for eirls under 10, 100 yards race for boys from 10 to 15, 80 yards race for girls from 10 to 15, 50 yards egg and spoon race far females, three-legged race for boys under 16, skipping-rope race for girls, 50 yards human wheelbarrow race, 50 yards race for boys and girla under,7, picking up potatoes race,jumble boot race. 150 yards flat race, 50 yards sack race, 150 yards slow bicycle race, obstacle race, tug-of-war (eight a-side). 50 yards race for ladies over 40 and under 50,80 yards race for gentlemen ever 40 and under 50, 50 yards race for ladies over 50, 50 yards race for gentlemen over 50. Tea was provided in the pariah room for the children and in the Temperance Hall for the aged people. The catericiv, was in the hands of Mr James Horton, the Park Bakery, and it gave every satisfaction. The proceedings were wound up with a torchlight proceesion at nine o'clock ending with the National Anthem, sung by the united assembly. A meted of praise is due to the committee and their energetic hots secretary (Mr w L Benner) for the excellent arrangements which had been made. PENKETI3.I

NEWTON URBAN DISTRICT COUNCIL.

THE SANITARY ARRANGEMENTS.

A meeting of the Urb in District Council of Newton-in- Makerfield was held in the Town Hall, Earlestown, on Monday evening. Mr J W Gollinwood presided and there were also present : Messrs C B F Borron, W J Sirnpson,W Thompson, Dr Latham, with C Cale (clerk), A Bowes (surveyor), and ft T Suttees (water engineer).

===== 0002601_19020815_art0114.txt | included=true | included =====
THE CROWNING OF THE KING.

The Chairman thought before proceeding with the business of the day the Council ought to record their heartfelt thanks to the Giver of 411 Rood things for their beloved King's happy recovery and at the same time formally give expression to their joy and exultation that the Coronation had at length been successfully carried through and that their King and Queen bad been duly crowned according to our most ancient rights and that his Majesty had been able to bear the fatigue of the ceremony withoutlany injury b his health May they live long to rule over this Empire, and may, they, his subjects loyally and faithfully do their duty. (Hear,;hear.) He moved that a resolution to that effect be recorded on the minutes.

Mr Gdmour, in seconding the proposition, said the King was the most popular man in England at the present time. The resolution was unanimously agreed to.

THE GARDEN CITY ASSOCIATION.

Dr. Latham asked whether the Council was repre- seated at the housing conference held at Liverpool and Port Sunlight in connection with the Garden City Associatioo.

The Chairms.n said he had the pleasure of attendittg one of the gatherings at Port Sunlight. He was very much entertained by what he saw and what he heard and perhaps the knowledge gained might be of some use to the district in future. GAS COMMITTEE.

At a meeting of this Committee, the Engineer reported that Mr Francis Jones, F.R.S.E., Professor of Chemistry. Manchester, had made an examination of the gas sup- plied to consumers, and that Mr Janes had reported that he found the method of purification the best arrange- ment for the purpose, and that, after an analysis of the gas, he found that sulphuretted hydrogen and ammonia were entirely absent. The illuminating power he found to be 19 06 candles.—A letter was read from the Local Government Board enclosing their sanctions to the borrowing of the following sums for the purpose of the Council's gas undertaking, viz. :—£4,000 for the exten- sion of the retort house, and the provision of additional lifts for the gas holders and setting in the retort house (including the itetti of £396 for contingencies)—loan to be repaid within a period not exceeding 29 years from the date of the borrowing thereof. £2,060 in respect of new meters and street lamps (being three-fifths of the sum of £3,433 included in the estimate for these pur- pases)—loan to be repaid within 10 years. £1,500 in respect of new main.' and services (being three-fifths of the sum of £2,500 included in the estimate for these purposes)—to be repaid within 30 years.—The Local Government Board also stated that they had given the application of the Council for sanction to borrow the sum of £4,067, which had already been expended and included in closed and audited accounts, their further consideration, and that they bad decided, in the present instance, to sanction the borrowing of the amount in question—the loan to be repayable in 15 years.—As regards the amount required fur cooking st eves, the Board stated that a loan could not be sanctioned for that purpose.— A letter was read from Mr R Eaton with respect to the lighting of streets in the district, which letter was referred to the Chairman of the Gas Commit- tee for consideration.

Mr Thompson m wed the confirmation of the minutes and Mr Simpson seconded. Dr Latham asked if the report of Mr Francis Jones would be published in detail. Mr Thompson slid he had no objection to printing the report in full, if necessary. Dr Latham said he believed the Council had already incurred liabilities with respect to cooking stoves and IA the Local Government Board refused to sanction a loan, he wished to know what was being done in the matter. Mr Thompson said he had co doubt the Chairman of the Finance Committm would deal with the matter, WATER COMMITTEE.

At the meeting of this committee it was resolved that the question of the amount of water supplied to the Vulcan Foundry other than by meter be referred to the Chairman of the Finance Committee, and that the application of Mr I' Cooke (eneinemao) for an increase of wages was referred to the Staff Committee. It'veply to a question by Mr Gilmour, Mr Borron said a meeting of the Staff Committee would be held shortly when Mr Cooke's application would be considered. The minutes were passed. HIGHWAYS AND SANITARY COMMIT TEE. THE MEDICAL OFFICER'S REPORT.

The report of the Medical Officer of Healy, for the past quarter was read, viz :—The birth-rate was, 36.6, and the death-rate 14.6, per 1,000 of the estimated populr tion. The causes of death were certified as follow .—Diphtheria and membraneous croup, 2 ; diarrhoea 1 v.interitisi, 3 ; phtlthis, 5 ; cancer,malignant disease, 2 ; bronchitis, 5 ; pueurnonia, •10 ; heart diseases, 5 ; accidents, 3 ; suicide, 1 • influenza, 1 ; all other causes, 25. Total, 63. The infantile death-rate was 10.4 per coot, and the thiaths under five years of age were 31 7 per crat of the total number of deaths. Twenty-one cases of infectious disease (including 7 from puthisis) were notified to me. The committm resolved that the request of the Royal Commission on sewage disposal for this Council to treat the sewage at the central works day and night, be granted. 'floe tenders which had been recei red for the sewering, of part of Ashton-road (from crow-lane to Brooks-square) were :submitted Co the committee, and it was resolved that the tender of Mr. George Finning be accepted for the work, and that the Clerk be instructed to prepare the contract accordingly. The commtttee also resolved that the Council be recom- mended to direct the Clerk to make application to the Local Government Board for a provisional order to amend the Newton District Improvement Ace, 1855, which incorporates the Town's Improvement Clauses Act, 1847, so that on the erection of any new building, the Council shall be empowered to require that such new building shall tati provided with proper and sufficient water closets and waste water closets, or with one or more of either class of closet, according as circumstances may require. The Chairman moved the confirmation of the minutes.

?dr Borron, referring to the last named resolution,said it was rather too.early a resolution to pass at that meet- ing. Such a resolution would alter the existing sanitary arr augements and as there was not a superfluous supply of water at present. the proposed new system of water carriage would affect the supply very materially. The present system was efficitntly carried on and the death rate of the district showed that the sanitary arrange- ments were not in an uosatisfactory conditi on. Be thought they ahould stay their hands until they were in the position of having an amole supply of water r o that the sewers might be sufficiently flushed. There was also the question of dealing with the extra sewage which ought to be a matter for careful consideration before they decided upoa a water closet system. He moved that the matter be referred back.

Mr Gilmour said los was in favour of the proposed system being adopted,but as they were not in a position to supply the water required, be would second the reso- lution for the matter to be referred back.

Dr Latham said it appeared that som- members of the Council were a little slower than others in arriving at what be chose to call a satisfactory conclusion. He should have thoueht that the elaborate report which was submitted to the members by the Surveyor would have been sufficient for soy member of the Council to have made up his mind on the matter. Personally he (Dr Latham) bad made up his mind that the present sanitary sysiem was discreditable in the extreme. The system of emptying pails and replacing the same pail was a disgrace to any sanitary authority The Sanitary Inspector had furnished them with particulars of the cost of providing pails which had been kept clean and prepared ready for replacement. When that reporter's re id thereiwaa no one bold enough to suggest that the Council should incur the additional cost. The death rate of the district was certainly low in comparison with some of the large towns in the immediate district, hut he would remind them that the death rate in one district bore no comparison with the death rate in another, unless they were acquainted with local conditions which were responsible tor the death rate. He contended that the death rate of the district was not in a satisfactory condition. It was not as satisfactory as it might be and not what he hoped i would be. With regard to the extra deposits in the sewage by the use of water carriage, it had been proved beyond all possible doubt that the sewage of water closet towns was very little different frem that of towns without the system. The extra work at the sewage works would be infinitesimal.lf they had not a sufficent quantity of water tor the purpose then it was the duty of the Water Committee to increase the supuly. Mr Barron, in reply, said he found in the Surveyor's report which had been alluded to, the fellowic,g ear - tence :—"The low death rate is sufficient evidence that the present system is sa'isfactory. There are some ob- jectionable features of the system which might be miti- gated, but suggested improvements would bs outside the scope of my instruCtions." He (Mr Borron) would be very glad to hear of those suggested improvements. (Hear, hear.) Doctors and experts differed on the question. He ha i followed the reports on the health of the district for Rome time and on compering the death rate he found that it compared to advsntege with die- tricts where the water-carriage system had been adopted. He thought thee should be very careful before they departed from what had been successful hitherto. 1 The amendment, referring the minlate back, WAS carried and the minutes were then passed. Mr Simpson polnted out that Dr Lath= had spo'aerr onoe.

Dr Latham : I take it I have your permission to prc- ceed, Odr Chairman.

The Chairman : I don't see bow the question arises. We are not postponing the work. Mr Simpson : The doctor has been away from the meetings and does not understand it.

EAR LESTOWN & NEWTON.

DAMAGING A POTATO FIELD AT NEWTON. For doing damage to the extent of 5s to potatoes in a field belonging to Turner Rigby, farmer,Samuel Garner, a Haydock collier, was fined 203 and costa on Thursday at Newton Petty Se ssi on s. CHOIR PICNIC FROM NEWTON. On Saturday the choir boys of St Peter's Church, Newton, had their annual outing. In charge of the Rev .T Ryder, Mr It Leyland (organist), and Mr W Welsh they left Newton about 9 o'clock for Southport. After a short ramble about the beach dinner was partaken of at Thorpe's refreshment rooms, and in the afternoon the lads had a abort sea trip. Tea was served at Thorpe's and heartily enjoyed and home was reached about 9 30, after a very pleasant outing.

ASSAULT ON A HAYDOCK GULL. At Newton Petty Sessions on Thursday George Berry, a collier, was charged with assaulting a girl named Margaret Davies, aged fifteen. The defend- ant did not appear. The complainant stated that on the 6th inst,as she was going along a lonely road in Haydock the defendant came up and attempted to criminally assault her.—Two witnesses gate evidenct. as to Davies making complaints to then/ of Berry's conduct.—The Bench inflicted a fine of 40s and costs.

THE ANNUAL HOLIDAYS. During Friday, Saturday and Monday last, in consequence of all the works in the district being closed for the annual ti oliday, Earlestown wore it quiet and deserted appearance. On Thursday even- ing the passenger station was most animated, the platforms being crowded until a late hour with people hastening away to their homes and friends, whilst many were journeying to Ireland and other places of popular resort, bent upon a holiday. On the succeeding days the station wore a busy aspect and the streets were proportionately quiet. So far as the weather is concerned the holiday, has heel very. enjoyable. There have been occasiona` showers. but Saturday and Monday ware delightful. At Earleatown the elements have not been very favourable, but those who have been long distances report that they have had an excellent time. An enormous number of privilege tickets were issued at the Viaduct Works but the amount of money received is some- what below that of last year. The works re-opened on Tuesday morning for bugine.s, but nearly soo- men were absent from dip Viaduct works and con- sequently those who ha U not previously obtained leave, were given an extended holiday (much against their wish) uatil the end of the week.

NOTES ON GAMES AND ATHLETICS, Cricket Warrington were well on the way to victory again last Saturday, when Cheetham were the visitors to the Arpley ground—and compiled the respectable total of 161, four men getting well into double figures. IVar- rington made a gallant effort to score the required runs in a little over an hour and a half, but the task was too heavy, and they were 29 behind with five men out when stumps were drawn. Connor and Hawtin opened the home innings, but , the younger of the two batsmen -was soon dismissed for I three, but so well did E A Connor and Lynes bat after the fall of P B Hawtin's wicket that there seemed every likelihood of the locals gaining a victory. Runs came at an exceedingly fast rate, but at 70 Lynes was adjudicated leg before, and ten runs later Connor was caught from a mis-hit. WII Podmore hit out merrily, and the chances acra'n went in favour of the Warring- tonians, but with 6Podmore's and Briggs's dismissal at 114, steady play by B Silcock and Bibby was witnessed, the total being 132 at the close of play. Silcock was not out 15, and Bibby 13 not out. By the way Connor's 38 was the highest score in thei match, just beating W Parker's. 37 for Cheetham. Lynes 23, and Podmore 29, both played creditable inn- ings. The latter is having quite a successful season. The second elevens of the same clubs were engaged at Cheetham, where Warrington scored a capital vic- tory. Though they lost 6 wickets for 31 runs at the start, the total eventually reached 97 thanks to a hard hit 41 by the captain,' E Pierpoint who was credited with a six and three fours. Plinston tollowed up his captain's batting success by some really fine bowling and obtained nine of the wickets for 25 runs, the Cheet- ham total being 59, of which A Hawthorn compiled 31 not out. The other wicket fell to the fielders, White- head being run out. Plinston deserved to have secured tho whole ten—a by no means common occurrence , even in club cricket. -- Penketh endeavoured to " take in and do for" War- rington Wesleyans on Saturday but:were not quite equal to the task, the scores being 38 and 49, for the respec- tive teams. Innings of 15 and 13 by Evans and A. Highley went far toward securing the victory for the Wesleyans. No man reached , double figures on the home side. -- Another creditable performance has to be put to the credit of Sankey Wire Mills who decisively defeated Knotty Ash on their own ground. J. Evans took six wickets for seven runs on behalf of the wire-millers,who like the Australians, may lay full claim to the " Ashes" I (Knotty). Moore and Daresbury proved too strong for'Crosfield's men at Moore, compiling 100 to the latters 29. E. E. Woods of the Warrington club rattled up 20 and took four wickets for 11 runs, the Rev F. C. Smith scored 29 not out. T. Forster bowled well for the visitors, his average being four wickets for 11 runs. ---- " Married v. Single " was the title of a match organised by Messrs Fletcher Russell's office staff and played on the Grappenlaall ground. The single men compiled 37 against the !benedicts' 15, and the latter perforce, have to hide their diminished heads for a while. Does marria.ie improve one's batting ? is question they might be tempted to discuss. Rugby Football. The draw for the first round of the Cup Competition was carried out on Tuesday evening in Manchester with the result that Warrington visit St Helens and Runcorn are at home to Widnes. It was resolved, prior to the draw, that the clubs are arranged in districts, i.e 1, Cumberland ; 2, Furness district ; 3, Rest of Lanca- shire ; 4, West Yorks ; 5, East Yorks ; 6, Durham ; Warrington and the other South West Lancashire clubs figure in Section 3. The first round will be a "teaser " for many of the clubs and beside the local games above mentioned, Broughton Rangers and Oldham will meet at Wheater's Field. Batley, by the way, have their usual luck, Sowerby Bridge (away) being their destined victims. It is rather curious that the South-West Lancashire clubs should all come together in the first round—and not altogether fortunate—for the fate of two of them will soon be settled, which two the coming season's form will perhaps indicate. Runcorn and Warrington should be the professors but " one never knows." In recent years St Helens have proved rather easy victims for Warrington, who won there in a cup-tie not long ago. 'The success may be a happy augury. +Ow Good Templary at Warrington. The weekly meeting of the Temperance Mission Lodge of the I 0 G T was held on Wednesday even- ing in the Ebenezer Misson Room, Buttermarket- street. Bro F Merlin, CT. presided over a good attendance, visitors being present from Earlestown, Latchford, and Wrexham. Bro C J Sheath, Sec, gave a brief report of the meeting of No 1 Convention held at Runcorn last Saturday. Bro E Newman, DS J T, gave a short address on the Juvenile question and urged the lodge to try and form a temple in connection with the lodge. Sister Woods, C S J T, supported him and ultimate- ly the lodge decided to try and a strong Juvenile Executive Committee was formed. An interesting programme was arranged for next quarter and was ordered to be sent to the publishers of the Guide. The programme for the evening was "New officers responsible," and the following items were given : _Bro W Griffiths, cornet solo; Sister S L Sheath song, "Beautiful Isle of the sea"; Sister H E Wake. field recitation, "Is this a home ? " Sister Bowyer song, " The song that reached my heart " ; Bro A Egerton, mouth-organ solo: Sister G Hughes recitation, "Rover in Church " ; Bro C J Sheath song.' Rocked in the cradle of the deep ; " Bro F Merlin, reading; Sister F Millington ode, " May I come in"; Bro J Sheath humorous speech, 'Troubles of a Chinese Ambassador." The lodge was closed in ,due form by Bro Merlin, C T.

OUR LOCAL CHURCHES. Wycliffe P. S. A. Society. The usual meeting of this Society was held on Say day afternoon. Mr B Farrar presided and spoke upon " Welker Brethren," and counselled his hearers t 3 " Watch and Pray." Mr Bithgate, of Liverpool. delivered an address on " The Fall of Man," referring to the forbidden tree not as a trial tree, but as a warning tree. He urged upon his hearers to note the weak pointe of their natures and avoid evil. Mr W H Payton, MO. Bac., played as an organ solo a Coronation March. the close, Mr Farrar referred to the crowning of tbe King and Queen on the previous day, and wished long life to their Majesties. Be expressed the hope that tbe King and Queen would inspire the nations of the earth with Christian principles. The members heartily joined in singing the National Anthem. Mr A Healey and 3if Downes also took part in the meeting. Padgate Wesleyan Church. The anniversary in connection with this church yrs° celebrated on Sunday. The Rev J W Butcher, chairman of the Scotland district, occupied the pulpit. In the morning special Coronation hymns were sung and tbe anthem " Send out Thy Light," was well rendered. I° the evening the choir gave the anthem, "Glory to Thee, ,my God, this night." In the afternoon Dr Bennett pre- sided over a musical service, given by the Apollo Mal,e Voice Choir, under the leadership of Mr T Brough. very interesting programme was given. The Rev J Butcher gave a short address to the children. By request the anthem given in the morning was repeated after the evening service. The collections amounted to £I3 135' The Rev, C. H. Hocken's Farewell. On Sunday morning the Rev C ET Hocken (who leaving the circuit for Derby) preached his fareavel„ sermon at the Wesleyan Chapel, Penketb. He thankeo the members and friends for the sympathy and kindial which bad been extended to him on all hands. Mr " Parker, on behalf of the society, wished the rev gentle, man "God speed in his new sphere of labour. 11 commemoration of the Coronatioo the pulpit was draPeo with the Union Jack. The congregation sang tA, National Anthem. The Rev C H Hocken will close In' ministry at Bewsey-road Wesleyan Church tc-Morrovi (Sunday) evening, Shrewsbury Great Floral Fete. What is described as " the most magnifieell flower show ever seen in Great Britain " will be held at Shrewsbury on Wednesday and ThursdaYl August 20th and 21st, when the annual floral f will take place. A host of attractions will be Pr? 'Tided. Three bands have been'engaged, H.M. Royal Horse Guards (Blue), IJ.M. Coldatrearn Guards and MM. Royal Marines (Portsmouth) There will be sensational performances in ate grounds by expert artistes and other attractio° will include balloon ascents, a horse leaping COO petition,illuminations and fireworks. Special excar_ sion trains to Shrewsbury will be run from 00 district. of At Newmarket on Tuesday, Lydia Jennings, wife an artilleryman serving in South Africa, was comnyitte; for trial charged with the murder of her son Alfred,age' three, at Burwell, near Newmarket. The evidel showed that the accused was not in want, but waS fond of drink. The child was found lying dead on a b,e, with its throat cur, and the prisoner admitted havilry, committed the deed, adding that she was obliged to "" it, as she had not a friend in the world.

An Early Warrington Ordination. The Record Society has recently published the first Ordination Book of the diocese of Chester (1542-1553), containing the names of the clergy ordained during this period and their " titles," which were generally nominations by local gentry. The volume is thus of much interest to the local historian, and it has also a bearing on the general history of a stormy period. Among the names of lspecial note may be mentioned those of two clergy who took opposite sides in the great struggle of toe time. James Pilkingtun, of Rivington, ordained acolyte and sub-deacon in September, 1543, became an extreme Protestant, fled the country in Mary's reign, and returning in the next was made Bishop I of Durham. James Bell,of Warrington,was ordained I priest in Lent, 1547 ; conformed, "against his con- science " as he afterwards owned,to the Elizabethan order, and served thus for 20 years ; then serious illness brought repentance, and the change being noticed he was executed in 1584 for his rejection of the Queen's spiritual supremacy. As an illustra- tion of this supremacy it may be stated that the titles of the lists in Henry Vlll.'s time usually state that the ordaining bishop was" lawfully empowed by the royal authority." Warrington P.S.A. Society. The weekly meeting was held in the Co-operative Hall, Sankey-street, on Sunday. There was an excellent attendance. The Rev T Bishop, whose ministry in Warrington is drawing to a close paid his last visit, and gave a much appreciated address upon the P.S.A. motto: " Bear ye one another's burdens." A hearty welcome was accorded , Messrs Boyle and Dickinson, two members of the society who recently returned from South Africa With the Volunteer contingent. Another Prizefor the Borough Band. On Monday the Borough Band secured the Ist prize—Xls at the Rhos annual contest. A splendid performance of the test piece "Echoes of t'3e Alps " was given, which from its title should prove brim- ful of pretty effects. The fact that the contest took place in Wales adds all the more to the merit of the success for, musically speaking, they do things well in Wales. COMIERCYAL FAlLllRES.—According to" Benin's mer- cantile Gazette," the number of New Bankruptcy Pro- ceedings in England and Wales gazetted during the ' week ending August 9th, was 79. The number in the corresponding week of last year was 54, Ebowing an increase of 25, being a net decrease in 1902, to date, or 38.
